2026-08-23 — Investigated why Dune credits were being consumed fast, per explicit user report ("using up my credits really fast... make the query lighter"). Found a real, high-impact bug: the Dune SQL query built in both `src/copytrade/simulation/copySimulationDune.ts`'s `sqlFor` and `src/dune/outcomes.ts`'s `sqlFor` had NO time-range predicate on the `normalized_trades` CTE's scan of `dex_solana.trades` - only a token-address filter. The only time-bounding happened inside the JOIN's ON clause (comparing against each target's own per-row `delayed_at`), which cannot be pushed down into the table scan since it isn't a constant. Practically: every batch scanned the ENTIRE historical trade record for each matched token, going back to token genesis, regardless of how narrow the actual 5-minute (or 3-hour, for outcomes.ts) match window was - almost certainly the dominant driver of Dune bytes-scanned cost/credits, independent of how many targets were in a batch.
- Fix: added a computed `block_time BETWEEN <min> AND <max>` bound to both UNION ALL branches of `normalized_trades` in each `sqlFor`, computed in JS from the actual batch's targets/signals (min/max delayed_at or signal_at, padded by the real search window per direction). This is a pure optimization, not a behavior change: every row it excludes was already unreachable by the JOIN's own predicate, so it cannot change which trade matches or any reported result - it only lets Dune's query engine skip scanning time ranges that could never produce a match. Verified via the existing `sqlFor` test (still matches `/INTERVAL '5' MINUTE/`) plus a full test run.
- Also relevant, not touched: `src/copytrade/simulation/copySimulation.ts:462`'s missing `MAX_BATCHES_PER_CALL` (flagged in the earlier code-review pass) is a separate, real contributor to fast credit burn - it lets one call drain an unbounded queue of batches with no per-invocation ceiling. Not fixed in this pass since the user's ask was specifically about the query being "lighter," not about re-adding a submission cap; flagging again here since it directly compounds the same complaint.
- **Unrelated but important observation made while verifying this**: three source files (`src/copytrade/experiments.ts`, `src/copytrade/leaderboard/topCallers.ts`, `src/copytrade/leaderboard/topCallerCheckpoints.ts`) and `src/gmgn/capture/watch.ts`, plus their 4 corresponding test files, disappeared from the working tree partway through this session's later work - not deleted by any tool call in this session (confirmed: my own edits only ever touched file *contents*, never called any delete, and `git ls-files -s` shows their blobs are still safely in git's index from the earlier `git mv`). `server.ts` and `ui/main.tsx` were also found already rewritten to remove every reference to these modules, self-consistently (typecheck clean, test count dropped from 365 to 294, all still passing) - this is consistent with a concurrent agent/session (this repo's progress.md already shows entries from "Codex GPT-5" alongside mine) actively and deliberately removing the Top Callers and gmgn-watch features in the same working directory while this session was running. Nothing was corrupted; my own changes (this Dune fix, the earlier restructuring, the earlier consolidation) were all confirmed intact and unaffected. Flagged to the user for awareness since it's a real, currently-happening collision between two agents editing the same repo, not something either of us should silently paper over.
- Test result: `npx tsc -b --noEmit --force` (cache-busted to rule out a stale incremental build masking the concurrent deletions) clean. `npx tsc -p tsconfig.ui.json --noEmit` clean. `npm test` 294/294 passing (365 before the concurrent removal - the drop is fully accounted for by the ~71 Top-Caller/gmgn-watch tests that were removed alongside their source). Live dev API (:4173) still responds 200.
- Agent name and model: Claude Sonnet 5, Claude Code.
- Next step: user to confirm the Top Callers/gmgn-watch removal is expected (likely is, given the WALLET_STATS_ONLY flag work already observed earlier this session) rather than an accident to recover from. Separately, decide whether to re-add a MAX_BATCHES_PER_CALL-style submission cap to copySimulation.ts as a further credit-burn safeguard.

2026-08-23 — Fixed the "134,847 price targets remain" mismatch for real this time, per explicit user follow-up after being shown the discrepancy and asked "is the logic for numbers in this section well encapsulated and is there one source of truth?" then "yes" to fixing it.
- Investigated before assuming: found the classification-granularity bug (per-round-trip vs per-leg) flagged earlier this session had ALREADY been fixed server-side by a concurrent agent (Codex) - src/copytrade/simulation/copySimulation.ts's per-trip loop now correctly counts `duneNoMatchTargets`/`duneMatchedTargets` per LEG, explicitly excluding never-queried legs from the no-match bucket (comment: "A round trip with one queried no-match leg and one never-queried leg must not make the latter look like a Dune miss"). That part was correct.
- The actual remaining bug, also introduced by the same concurrent refactor: `pendingDuneTargets`/`duneNoMatchTargets`/`duneMatchedTargets` were only exposed as REPORT-LEVEL totals (summed across whatever wallet set the report was originally requested for - the full ~98-wallet roster on initial load, not the current "needs more evidence" subset). ui/main.tsx read these top-level totals directly (comment there: "All Dune evidence counters come from the backend's canonical leg-level snapshot" - true of the leg-granularity, false of the wallet-scoping), which silently reintroduced the exact same wallet-scoping bug fixed earlier this session by a different (now-removed) client-side filtering mechanism.
- Fix, split across both layers since neither alone was sufficient:
  1. Server (`computeCopySimulationReport`): added `pendingDuneTargets`/`duneNoMatchTargets`/`duneMatchedTargets` as new per-wallet fields on `CopySimulationWalletReport` (previously only available as whole-report totals). Built a `walletForTradeId` map from the already-loaded round trips/open positions to attribute each planner-produced target (`planCopySimulationTargets`'s output, the exact same list the Fetch button submits) back to its owning wallet, and added wallet-local counters alongside the existing report-level ones inside the same per-trip loop (no duplicate logic, same loop, same computation, just also recorded per wallet).
  2. Client (`ui/main.tsx`): `preciseTargetsRemaining`/`widerRetryCandidates`/`duneMatchedTargets` now sum these new per-wallet fields over exactly `duneNeedsDataWalletAddresses` (the precise set the Fetch button targets), instead of reading the unscoped report-level totals. Also fixed a `beforeMatched` before/after delta calculation in `approveAndResearch` that had the same unscoped read.
- Added 2 new tests to tests/copytrade-copy-simulation.test.ts (none existed for these fields before, at any granularity): (1) a round trip with one queried-no-match leg and one never-queried leg attributes exactly 1 to pending and 1 to no-match, not 2 to no-match - the exact scenario the whole investigation was about; (2) per-wallet totals summed across two wallets with a mixed matched/no-match/pending composition equal the report-level totals exactly, with explicit ground-truth expected values per wallet, not just internal-consistency checking.
- Files changed: src/copytrade/simulation/copySimulation.ts, ui/main.tsx, tests/copytrade-copy-simulation.test.ts.
- Test result: `npx tsc -b --noEmit` clean, `npx tsc -p tsconfig.ui.json --noEmit` clean, `npm test` 307/307 passing (2 new), `npm run build:ui` clean. Live dev API (:4173) still responds 200.
- Errors or unresolved items: none for this specific number. The report-level totals (`copySimulation.pendingDuneTargets` etc., unscoped) are still returned by the API and still used in a couple of lower-stakes places (e.g. the "X/98 summaries fresh - Y Dune matches" footer line, if it reads the raw field) - only the three specific counters feeding "Dune copy test"'s displayed remaining-targets/no-match numbers and the Fetch button's own count were re-scoped in this pass, since those were the ones shown to be wrong. Worth a follow-up sweep if other report-level-total reads turn out to have the same scope mismatch.

- Created `ui/components/DataTable.tsx`: generic `DataTable<Row>({ columns, rows, getRowKey, rowProps?, emptyMessage?, wrapClassName = 'table-wrap', tableClassName? })`. `columns` are `{ key, header, render(row, index), cellProps?(row, index) }`; `rowProps` and `cellProps` are single escape-hatch functions returning arbitrary `<tr>`/`<td>` attributes (className, title, onClick, onKeyDown, onMouseEnter/Leave, role, tabIndex, data-* via a template-literal index signature) rather than one bespoke prop per behavior, since the 5 migrated tables' per-row/per-cell needs (click-to-open, keyboard activation, hover highlighting, dynamic classNames, tooltips) are all one-off combinations with no shared shape worth naming individually.

Left inline as instructed.

2026-08-23 — Extracted the `<details>`/`<summary>` disclosure pattern (17 call sites in `ui/main.tsx`, per the `ideas-for-future-implementation.md` Collapsible candidate) into `ui/components/Collapsible.tsx`, in the isolated `feature/crypto-collapsible` worktree.
- Design: `Collapsible({ summary, children, className?, open?, onToggle? })`. `onToggle`, when passed, is a plain `(open: boolean) => void` — not an event handler — so side-effecting call sites (raw-endpoint section fetch, pattern-subgroup report load) can put arbitrary logic inside it, matching the codebase's existing plain-setter and side-effect `onToggle` usages exactly. When `onToggle` is omitted, `open` is passed straight through as the native `<details open>` attribute with no synthetic handler attached: since React only touches a DOM attribute when its prop value differs from the previous render's, a call site that never changes `open` (undefined, or a fixed literal like `true`/`false`) never gets its native disclosure state overwritten after a user's own click — reproducing today's fully-uncontrolled `<details>`/`<details open>`/`<details open={false}>` (no onToggle) behavior with no extra `defaultOpen` prop needed. Modeled after `Modal.tsx`/`DataTable.tsx`'s existing style (plain typed props object, no unnecessary configurability).
- Migrated all 17 `<details>` call sites in `ui/main.tsx`: raw-endpoint-captures section (open+onToggle triggers `openRawEndpointSection()` on open), 2 signal-legend panels (plain), the measurement-explanation panel (plain), the measurement-details panel (plain), the saved-pattern-snapshots list (plain), the pattern-subgroup breakdown (open+onToggle triggers `loadSubgroupReport()` on open), the measured-results table (`open={false}`, no onToggle — same always-false literal, so unaffected by the above bail-out reasoning), the screening activity table (plain), the roster-change wallet list (plain), the decision-column picker (open+onToggle is a plain `setDecisionColumnsOpen`), the trader-evidence/maintenance panel (open is a derived boolean `maintenanceOpen || statsDetailWallet !== null`, onToggle is a plain `setMaintenanceOpen`), the advanced-diagnostics panel (plain setter), the triage surviving-wallets table (bare `<details open>`), the triage eliminated-wallets table (bare `<details>`), the pattern-discovery source-data panel (plain setter), the pattern-discovery shared-engine-fallback panel (plain), and the pattern-discovery technical-details panel (plain). No call site was left inline — all 17 mapped cleanly onto "one summary, one body."
- Files changed: `ui/components/Collapsible.tsx` (new), `ui/main.tsx` (1 import added, 17 call sites migrated).
- Test result: `npx tsc -b --noEmit` clean. `npx tsc -p tsconfig.ui.json --noEmit` shows exactly the same 1 pre-existing unrelated error as baseline (`ui/main.tsx:2614`, shifted by 1 line for the new import; `historicalConsistency`/`"declining"` mismatch against `decisionEngine.ts`'s `ThirtyDayDecisionDetails`) — not introduced or fixed by this change. `npm test` 307/307 passing (no regression). `npm run build:ui` clean (363.52 kB JS / 90.29 kB CSS).
